Heavenly Poisson bracket

Next, consider the heavenly case.
The
expansions truncate at linear order
if we have the zig vertex only.
The Poisson bracket is
exactly
given as

\displaystyle\omega^{-1}=\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}\,.

(44)

The holomorphic massive twistor space
{\mathbb{MT}}
remains to be a Lagrangian submanifold,

\displaystyle\{Z_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I},Z_{\mathrm{B}}{}^{J}\}=0\,,\quad\{Z_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I},{\bar{Z}}_{J}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\}=-i\hskip 1.00006pt\delta_{J}{}^{I}\delta_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\,,

(45)

while the anti-holomorphic
{\mathbb{MT}}^{*}
becomes Poisson-noncommutative as

\displaystyle\{{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}},{\bar{Z}}_{J}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\Delta}\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon_{IJ}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{\smash{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\dot{{\delta}}}}\vphantom{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pt\rho^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}\rho^{{\dot{{\delta}}}{\mathrm{B}}}\,,

(46)

where
\rho^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}
is the “converter”
that follows from
pulling
dz^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
back from
\mathbb{CE}^{1,3}
to
{\mathbb{MT}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}:=\begin{pmatrix}-iz^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\alpha}&\delta^{\dot{{\gamma}}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}{\quad\implies\quad}&dz^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}=-i(\rho\hskip 1.00006ptdZ\lambda^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,,\quad\rho^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}Z_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}=0\,.\end{split}

(47)

For reference, we unpack (
46
) in the hybrid basis:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}},{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{J{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\}&=(2/\Delta)\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon_{IJ}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}(z)\,,\\
\{{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}},{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\}&=(4i/\Delta)\hskip 1.00006pt({\bar{\lambda}}^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}J}\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon_{IJ}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{{\delta}}}}\vphantom{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\delta}}}\beta}\,,\\
\{{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha},{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\}&=(8/\Delta{\bar{\Delta}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\epsilon}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\phi_{\smash{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\dot{{\delta}}}}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\alpha}y^{{\dot{{\delta}}}\beta}\,.\end{split}

(48)

From the candy-shaped diagram

,
one intuitively sees that
the “zig” symplectic perturbation induces Poisson non-commutativity on the “zag” massive twistor space.
For the particle to be minimally coupled,
the self-dual field strength should only appear in
\{{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}},{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{J{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\}
among the spinorial frame brackets,
and hence we find (
34
).

Heavenly equation of motion

Further, let us check the minimal spin precession behavior by deriving the equation of motion with (
34
).
For the constant-mass case,
(
28
) gives

\displaystyle\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{d}{d\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{-2}\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\right)+\frac{1}{-2}\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\right)\,.

(49)

{\bar{\Delta}}
and
\Delta
generate holomorphic and anti-holomorphic translations in the free theory:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{{\,\,\,\,\,},{\bar{\Delta}}\}^{\circ}=\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}={\bar{\Delta}}(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{z}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}\,,\quad\{{\,\,\,\,\,},\Delta\}^{\circ}=\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}=\Delta(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{{\bar{z}}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}\,.\end{split}

(50)

The interaction with

\omega^{\prime+}

performs a
local
chiral (complexified) Lorentz transformation:

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}

\displaystyle=\frac{-2}{\Delta}\hskip-0.50003pt\left({\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{I{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}(z)\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}+2i\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{\beta}}\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}\right)\,.

(51)

Note that
2i\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\partial/\partial{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}|_{z}=-\partial/\partial y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}|_{z}
.
As
{\mathbb{MT}}
is Poisson-commutative, the zig sector of the particle gets decoupled from the interaction:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{Z}_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}&={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}\left\langle dZ_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I},\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\right\rangle\,,\\
\dot{{\bar{Z}}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}&={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}\left\langle d{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}},\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\right\rangle+{\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}\left\langle d{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}},-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\right\rangle\,.\end{split}

(52)

The angle bracket here represents the contraction between one-forms and vectors.
The linear coupling in the
\dot{{\bar{Z}}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}
equation of motion encodes all changes in the dynamics due to
\omega^{\prime}
,
while
\omega^{\prime}
need not be “small.”
Such linearization or exact solvability is indeed the characteristic of heavenly physics.

Minimal spin precession

From (
50
)-(
52
),
we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{\lambda}_{\alpha}{}^{I}=0&\,,\quad\textstyle\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}=\frac{1}{m_{0}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{I{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I}}\,\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}(z)\,,\\
\dot{z}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}m_{0}(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}&\,,\quad\textstyle\dot{{\bar{z}}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}m_{0}(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}+\frac{1}{m_{0}}\hskip 1.00006pt2i\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{\beta}}\alpha}\,.\end{split}

(53)

In turn, it follows that the
precession of the right-handed spin frame
{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
and the precession of spin length pseudovector
y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
are
exactly
synchronized:

\displaystyle i\Big({\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{x}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}-{\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}m_{0}(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}\Big)\,=\,\dot{y}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hskip 1.00006pt=\,\textstyle-\frac{1}{m_{0}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi{}^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{\beta}}\alpha}\,.

(54)

Thus, we conclude that the prescription (
34
) “universally” leads to the minimal spin precession behavior,
regardless of what specific field
\phi
describes!

Figure 1:

Geometric characterization of the gyromagnetic ratio
g
.
A charged spinning particle exhibits both orbital and spin precession in an external “magnetic” field.
If
g{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}2
, the two precessions are locally synchronized;
such a motion appears as an isotropic spiral trajectory as seen in the particle’s spin-space-time.
In contrast,
g{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}0
translates to a “flattened” sinusoidal curve.
Notice how spin-space-time offers a new angle on spin precession.
The visual reasoning suggests that the field strength should somehow extend into the complex.
The isotropy of
g{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}2
points to the equal blending of all
dx
-
dy
components in
dz{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz
;
the “flatness” of
g{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}0
points to the reality of
dx{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx
.

Earthly equation of motion and
\bm{C_{\ell}{\,\hskip 1.15005pt=\hskip 1.15005pt\,}1}

Finally, we spell out the constant-mass earthly equation of motion at
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\prime 1})

in the vectorial language by taking “
2\hskip 0.50003pt{\operatorname{Re}}
” to (
53
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{p}_{m}\simeq-\hskip 1.00006ptp_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt(\hskip 0.50003pt\phi^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(z){\,+\,}\phi^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 0.12497pt)^{n}{}_{m}&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\Lambda}^{m}{}_{a}\simeq\hskip 1.00006pt(\hskip 0.50003pt\phi^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(z){\,+\,}\phi^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 0.12497pt)^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda\hskip-0.50003pt^{n}{}_{a}\,,\\
m_{0}\dot{x}^{m}\simeq p^{m}-\hskip 1.00006pt*(\hskip 0.50003pt\phi^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(z){\,+\,}\phi^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 0.12497pt)^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{y}^{m}\simeq\hskip 1.00006pt(\hskip 0.50003pt\phi^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(z){\,+\,}\phi^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 0.12497pt)^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,.\end{split}

(55)

The orbital (
p_{m}
) and spin (
\Lambda\hskip-0.50003pt^{m}{}_{a}
,
y^{m}
)
precessions
are synchronized at

\mathcal{O}(\phi^{1})

(Figure
1
).
Taylor-expanding
the fields
around the real section,
we recognize

C_{\ell}{\,\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\,}1

(see (
183
)),
in particular

g{\,=\,}2

at
\mathcal{O}(y^{1})

and

\kappa{\,=\,}1

at
\mathcal{O}(y^{2})

by referring to TBMT and MPTD
equations.

Spin as a chiral Wick rotation of deviation

Meanwhile, note that
the evolution of
x^{m}

deviates from this synchronized precession (
p^{m}
)
by a zitterbewegung that is “dual” to the spin precession (
-{\ast\phi}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}
).
7
7

7

Note that the covariant spin gauge eliminates zitterbewegung in the free theory.

This zitterbewegung reflects the “spin-induced spacetime noncommutativity” (
15
) and its resolution in the complex combinations:
The covariant spin gauge fixing has put

deviation
in real spacetime and
spin

on an equal footing.
We give a closer look at this
dual
relationship between deviation and spin
in
[
83
]
.

3.2
Non-minimal equation of motion

We now return to the generic
class of
spin-space-time symplectic perturbations (
31
).

Maximally non-minimal coupling

A typical example of a non-holomorphic
spin-space-time
symplectic perturbation
is a closed two-form that localizes on the spacetime:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime+}&={\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\,}\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{x}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{x}^{n}}\,,\\
&={\textstyle\frac{1}{4}\,}\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\big({\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}+dz^{m}{\hskip 0.50003pt\otimes\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt}d{\bar{z}}^{n}+d{\bar{z}}^{m}{\hskip 0.50003pt\otimes\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt}dz^{n}+{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{{\bar{z}}}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{{\bar{z}}}^{n}}\big)\,,\\
&=:\frac{1}{4}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\,\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insert0+ww.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insert0+wb.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insert0+bw.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insert0+bb.pdf}\,\right)\,.\end{split}

(56)

In terms of (
31
), we have

\displaystyle{{}^{0}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{{}^{1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{{}^{2}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\,.

(57)

At first sight,
the symplectic perturbation
(
56
)
may seem like a “minimal” coupling prescription
because it simply
brings
the “field strength” two-form
without any post-processing,
such as the Newman-Janis shift.
However, in the holomorphic/anti-holomorphic basis,
it looks rather complicated.
In fact, the zig-zag representation discloses its
maximally non-minimal
nature.
We find three new types of zig-zag vertices,
which
cause a proliferation of zig-zag interaction scenarios:

\displaystyle\{{\,\,\,\,\,},{\bar{\Delta}}\}=\,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}

(58)

\displaystyle+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\cdots\vphantom{\hbox{\raise-8.61108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{$0$}}}}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\{{\,\,\,\,\,},\Delta\}=\,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}

(59)

\displaystyle+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\hskip-1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\hskip-1.00006pt+\cdots\,.

The expansions never truncate,
regardless of whether one assumes pure self-duality or not.
Besides, one can argue that

\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
actually contains an infinite number of derivative couplings as
\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)-iy^{r}\phi^{+}_{mn,r}(z)+\cdots

if one takes the holomorphic
Minkowski space
as more fundamental than the real spacetime.
In any respect, this is by no means “minimal.”

Maximally non-minimal spin precession:
\bm{C_{\ell\hskip 0.40251pt>\hskip 0.40251pt0}{\hskip 1.15005pt\,=\,\hskip 1.15005pt}0}

Let us compute the
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\prime 1})
equation of motion
that follows from (
56
).
First of all, we find

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+ww.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}

\displaystyle=\,\,\frac{(-2)}{\Delta}\,\omega^{\prime{\dot{\beta}}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}}(x)\hskip-1.00006pt\left({\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}+2iy^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\gamma}}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}{\gamma}}}\right)\,,

(60a)

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+wb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}

\displaystyle=\,\,\frac{(-2)}{{\bar{\Delta}}}\,\omega^{\prime{\dot{\beta}}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}}(x)\hskip-1.00006pt\left({\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}+2iy^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\gamma}}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}{\gamma}}}\right)\,,

(60b)

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bw.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}

\displaystyle=\,\,\frac{(-2)}{\Delta}\,p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 0.50003pt(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}-2iy^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\beta}}\right)\,,

(60c)

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/0+bb.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}

\displaystyle=\,\,\frac{(-2)}{{\bar{\Delta}}}\,p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 0.50003pt(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}-2iy^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\beta}}\right)\,.

(60d)

The first two lines are equal to (
51
) on the mass-shell constraint surface.
The last two lines involve an interesting combination
p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime{\dot{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}
.
This particular sandwiching has a geometrical interpretation as a reflection
across the momentum direction:

\displaystyle\begin{split}(p\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\sigma}}^{m}p)_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}=p^{2}\hskip 1.00006ptI^{m}{}_{n}(\sigma^{n})_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}&\,,\quad(p^{-1}\sigma^{m}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}=p^{-2}\hskip 1.00006ptI^{m}{}_{n}({\bar{\sigma}}^{n})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,,\\
(p\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\sigma}}^{mn}p^{-1})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}=-I^{m}{}_{r}I^{n}{}_{s}(\sigma^{rs})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}&\,,\quad(p^{-1}\sigma^{mn}\hskip 1.00006ptp)^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}=-I^{m}{}_{r}I^{n}{}_{s}({\bar{\sigma}}^{rs})^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\,,\\
I^{m}{}_{n}:=\delta^{m}{}_{n}-2p^{m}p_{n}/p^{2}&\,,\quad\varepsilon_{pqkl}\hskip 0.50003ptI^{p}{}_{m}I^{q}{}_{n}I^{k}{}_{r}I^{l}{}_{s}=-\varepsilon_{mnrs}\,.\end{split}

(61)

These identities are easily understood if
one recalls the inversive geometry of the
Clifford/conformal
algebra

[
170
]

and that the inversion map
p^{m}\mapsto p^{m}\hskip-0.50003pt/p^{2}
has the Jacobian
p^{-2}(\delta^{m}{}_{n}-2p^{m}p_{n}/p^{2})
.
For later use, we define
a notation “
{{}_{\mathcal{I}\hskip-0.81949pt}F_{mn}}
”
for
two-forms

F_{mn}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{}_{\mathcal{I}\hskip-0.81949pt}F_{mn}}:=F_{rs}\hskip 1.00006ptI^{r}{}_{m}I^{s}{}_{n}{\quad\implies\quad}\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rl}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006ptF^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}&=-(_{\mathcal{I}\hskip-0.81949pt}F)_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\,,\\
(p^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hskip 1.00006ptF_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}&=-(_{\mathcal{I}\hskip-0.81949pt}F)^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\,.\end{array}}\right.\end{split}

(62)

The heavenly equation of motion
follows from collecting
(
50
), (
60
), and (
62
).
Then the earthly equation of motion is then obtained by simply replacing
\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
with
\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
,
as the real perturbation
dx{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx
acts “symmetrically” on the zig and zag sectors:

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{p}_{m}\simeq-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi{}^{n}{}_{m}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\,,\quad&\textstyle m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\Lambda}^{m}{}_{a}\simeq-\frac{1}{{m_{0}}^{2}}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{m}p^{r}\phi_{rs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda\hskip-0.50003pt^{s}{}_{a}\,,\\
m_{0}\dot{x}^{m}\simeq p^{m}-(\eta^{mr}{-\hskip 1.00006pt}p^{m}p^{r}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt/p^{2})\hskip 1.00006pt{\ast\phi_{rs}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{s}\,,\quad&\textstyle m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{y}^{m}\simeq-\frac{1}{{m_{0}}^{2}}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{m}p^{r}\phi_{rs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{s}\,.\end{split}

(63)

(
63
) agrees with (
183
)
if
C_{\ell\hskip 0.40974pt>\hskip 0.40974pt0}{\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt}0
:

g{\,=\,}0
for TBMT
and
\kappa{\,=\,}0
for MPTD.
Evidently,
the precession behaviors of
p_{m}
and
y^{m}
are not synchronized.

To sum up,
our formalism is the simplest with the holomorphic symplectic perturbation (
34
)
and gets “maximally” complicated with (
56
):
all the
(2,0)
-,
(1,1)
-, and
(0,2)
-parts appear in (
56
)
with an “equal” magnitude.
This contrasts sharply
with the traditional approach,
which treats the real-supported symplectic structure (
56
) as the default
and implements the minimal gyromagnetic ratio
g{\,=\,}2

by an additional “non-minimal” spin interaction Hamiltonian
H^{\prime}=q\vec{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{S}

(and similarly for
\kappa
).
While the traditional approach
creates an unfortunate discrepancy between
the classical formalism and
the amplitudes-level understanding of
[
66
,
3
]
,
our perturbation theory
is literally minimal at the true minimal coupling

C_{\ell}{\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt}1

(
g{\,=\,}2
,
\kappa{\,=\,}1
,
\cdots
).
Therefore,
we argue that
our
“zig-zag”
formulation
provides a rationale for identifying

C_{\ell}{\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt}1

as the true minimal coupling
and

C_{\ell\hskip 0.40974pt>\hskip 0.40974pt0}{\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt}0

(
g{\,=\,}2
,
\kappa{\,=\,}0
,
\cdots
)
as the maximally non-minimal coupling
already at the classical level.
The massive twistor representation of massive spinning particles has guided us to do so,
as the zig-zag structure—already inherent in the
free theory
as the skew-diagonalization of the Poisson bracket—asserts that one should understand everything in the complex basis.

Generic heavenly equation of motion

Finally, we spell out the
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\prime 1})
heavenly equation of motion that follows from the generic perturbation (
31a
).
It is easily obtainable by recycling the calculations of (
60
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{d}{d\tau}\,\simeq\,\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{p^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}{-2}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}&{}+(\hskip 0.50003pt{{}^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}})^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip-0.50003pt\left({\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}+2iy^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\gamma}}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}{\gamma}}}\right)\\
&{}+({{}^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime}})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip-0.50003pt\left(-\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}+2iy^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}\beta}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(64)

where

{{}^{0+1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}:={{}^{0}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}+{{}^{1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}
,

{{}^{1+2}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}:={{}^{1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}+{{}^{2}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}
.
In other words,

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{\lambda}_{\alpha}{}^{I}\simeq-(^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{z}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\simeq{\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}{p^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}+2i\hskip 0.50003pty^{{\dot{\alpha}}\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt(^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime})_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}\simeq{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 0.50003pt(\hskip 0.50003pt^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime})^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{{\bar{z}}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\simeq{\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}{p^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}+2i\hskip 1.00006pt(\hskip 0.50003pt^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime})^{\dot{\alpha}}{}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{\beta}}\alpha}\,.\end{split}

(65)

In the vectorial language,
we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{p}_{m}&\simeq\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{{{}^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime+}}}-{{{}^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime+}}}\big)\hskip-0.50003pt{}_{m}{}^{n}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{n}\simeq-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime+}{}^{n}{}_{m}\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\Lambda}^{m}{}_{a}&\simeq\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{{{}^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime+}}}-{{{}^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime+}}}\big)\hskip-0.50003pt{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda\hskip-0.50003pt^{n}{}_{a}\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{y}^{m}&\simeq\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{{{}^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime+}}}-{{{}^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime+}}}\big)\hskip-0.50003pt{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,,\\
m_{0}\dot{x}^{m}&\simeq p^{m}-{\ast}\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{{{}^{0+1}\hskip-0.50003pt\omega^{\prime+}}}-{{{}^{1+2}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}\omega^{\prime+}}}\big)\hskip-0.50003pt{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,.\end{split}

(66)

Note that
I
and
\ast
anti-commute.

Arbitrary
g
value

Before moving on to the Wilson coefficients,
let us discuss two more examples for further concreteness.
First,
it is instructive to think of
a certain interpolation between the two extremes (
34
) and (
56
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime+}&{}=\phi_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}({x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2})\,\hskip 0.50003pt\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\hskip 1.00006ptd(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\,,\\
&{}=:{\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\hskip-0.50003pt\smash{(1+g/2)^{2}\,\hskip 1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insertg+ww.pdf}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-(g/2)^{2})\,\hskip 1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insertg+wb.pdf}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-(g/2)^{2})\,\hskip 1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insertg+bw.pdf}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-g/2)^{2}\,\hskip 1.00006pt\includegraphics[valign]{figs/insertg+bb.pdf}\hskip 1.00006pt\,}\bigg)}\,,\\
\omega^{\prime-}&{}=\phi_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}({x{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2})\,\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006ptd(x{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d(x{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\,,\\
&{}=:{\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\hskip-0.50003pt(1+g/2)^{2}\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\includegraphics[rotate]{figs/insertg+bb.pdf}}}}}}}}$}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-(g/2)^{2})\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\includegraphics[rotate]{figs/insertg+wb.pdf}}}}}}}}$}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-(g/2)^{2})\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\includegraphics[rotate]{figs/insertg+bw.pdf}}}}}}}}$}\hskip 1.00006pt+(1-g/2)^{2}\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\includegraphics[rotate]{figs/insertg+ww.pdf}}}}}}}}$}\hskip 1.00006pt\,\bigg)}\,.\end{split}

(67)

For reference,
we have spelled out the anti-self-dual counterpart as well.
As indicated in the diagrammatic format,
each part of
\omega^{\prime+}
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{}^{0}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}&\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\hskip 0.50003pt(1+g/2)^{2}\,\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)\,,\\
{{}^{1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}&\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\hskip 0.50003pt(1-(g/2)^{2})\,\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)\,,\\
{{}^{2}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}&\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\hskip 0.50003pt(1-g/2)^{2}\,\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)\,.\end{split}

(68)

The heavenly equation of motion (
66
) boils down to

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{p}_{m}&=-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}{}^{n}{}_{m}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt\dot{\Lambda}^{m}{}_{a}&=\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{\frac{g}{2}\,\phi^{+}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)-\frac{2{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}g}{2}\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,\phi^{+}{}^{l}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{n}{}_{a}\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{y}^{m}&=\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{\frac{g}{2}\,\phi^{+}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)-\frac{2{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}g}{2}\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,\phi^{+}{}^{l}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,,\\
m_{0}\dot{x}^{m}&=p^{m}+\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{-{\ast}\phi^{+}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)-\frac{2{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}g}{2}\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,{\ast}\phi^{+}{}^{l}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,.\end{split}

(69)

Replacing
\phi^{+}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}igy/2)

with
\phi^{+}{}^{m}{}_{n}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)+(g/2)\hskip 1.00006pt{\ast}((y{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\phi^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(x))^{m}{}_{n}+\cdots
,
one finds that (
69
) reproduces the
arbitrary-
g
TBMT equation (
181
)
in the small spin-derivative limit
(y{\hskip 0.50003pt\cdot\hskip 0.50003pt}\partial)\hskip 0.50003pt\phi\ll\phi
.
It is interesting that
the projector structure of the TBMT equation
derives from the reflection matrix
I^{m}{}_{n}
,
which traces back to the inversive geometry
of the Clifford algebra
(cf.
[
171
]
).

Antipodal minimal coupling

When we were introducing the ansatz (
34
),
the reader may have questioned the possibility of
extending the self-dual part anti-holomorphically:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime+}&\hskip 0.50003pt=\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\phi}}_{\smash{\alpha\beta}\vphantom{\beta}}({\bar{z}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}({d{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}})\,\,=\,\,\hskip 0.50003pt\includegraphics[valign,scale]{figs/insertA+.pdf}\hskip 0.50003pt\,,\\
\omega^{\prime-}&\hskip 0.50003pt=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}(z)\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon_{\smash{\alpha\beta}\vphantom{\beta}}({dz^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptdz^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}})\,\,=\,\,\hskip 0.50003pt\includegraphics[valign,scale]{figs/insertA-.pdf}\hskip 0.50003pt\,.\end{split}

(70)

In terms of (
67
),
this corresponds to
g{\,=\,}-2
.
The combinatorics of the zig-zag expansions
is the same as that of the minimal coupling:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega^{-1}=\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}\quad\text{(heavenly)}\,,\\
&{\begin{array}[]{rl}\omega^{-1}=\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}&{}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}-\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A-.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}\\
&{}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A-.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r.pdf}+\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/A-.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/r-b.pdf}+\cdots\quad\text{(earthly)}\,.\end{array}}\end{split}

(71)

We call this case “antipodal minimal coupling,”
in the sense that its equation of motion is a certain “inversion” of that of the minimal coupling.
The self-dual heavenly equation of motion reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{\lambda}_{\alpha}{}^{I}=-(^{\phantom{\hskip 0.2048pt\mathcal{I}}}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}{\bar{\phi}}({\bar{z}}))_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{z}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}{p^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}+2i\hskip 0.50003pty^{{\dot{\alpha}}\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt(^{\phantom{\hskip 0.2048pt\mathcal{I}}}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}{\bar{\phi}}({\bar{z}}))_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}=0&\,,\quad m_{0}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{{\bar{z}}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}{p^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}\,.\end{split}

(72)

One finds the same equations
by
taking complex conjugate to (
53
)
and then replacing

{\bar{\phi}}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})
with

(^{\phantom{\hskip 0.2048pt\mathcal{I}}}\mathllap{\hbox{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${}_{\mathcal{I}}\hskip 0.50003pt$}}}}}}}{\bar{\phi}}({\bar{z}}))_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}
.

Wilson coefficients on real worldlines

From the examples (
56
) and (
67
),
and further (
70
),
one can conjecture that (
31a
) has sufficient degrees of freedom to implement all types of
non-minimal
couplings
linear in the “field strength”:
Levi and Steinhoff
[
26
]
’s Wilson coefficients.

A scalar field
\phi(x)
on the real section of
\mathbb{CE}^{1,3}

holomorphically extends into the complex as
\Phi(x,y)

by the Cauchy-Riemann flow

\Phi(x,0)=\phi(x)
,

\partial\Phi/\partial y^{m}=i\hskip 1.00006pt\partial\Phi/\partial x^{m}

\implies

\Phi(x,y)=\exp(iy^{m}\partial/\partial x^{m})\hskip 1.00006pt\phi(x)=\phi(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}iy)
.
8
8

8

To avoid clutter,
we drop the complexification symbol
and denote
\phi^{\mathbb{C}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}iy)
just as
\phi(x{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}iy)
.

If the Cauchy-Riemann (holomorphy)
condition
{\partial\Phi\hskip-0.12497pt/\partial y^{m}}=i\hskip 1.00006pt{\partial\Phi\hskip-0.12497pt/\partial x^{m}}
is dropped,
the most general “analytic” continuation is then given by
replacing the exponential with a suitably convergent power series
as

\Phi(x,y)=\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{1+\frac{1}{1!}\hskip 1.00006ptC_{1}(iy^{m}\partial/\partial x^{m})+\frac{1}{2!}\hskip 1.00006ptC_{2}(iy^{m}\partial/\partial x^{m})^{2}+\cdots\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi(x)
.
This “non-holomorphic analytic continuation” equally applies to differential forms
by replacing

y^{m}\partial/\partial x^{m}
with
\pounds_{y}
,
where
y:=y^{m}\hskip 0.50003pt\partial/\partial x^{m}|_{y}
denotes the spin length as a vector field in
\mathbb{CE}^{1,3}
.
Then the constants
C_{1},C_{2},\cdots
are essentially the Wilson coefficients of
[
26
]
.

Hence,
we consider generic
symplectic perturbations of the form

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime\pm}=C^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\pm i\pounds_{y})\,[\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\pm}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{x}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{x}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,,\quad C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)=\textstyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}{\textstyle\frac{1}{\ell!}\hskip 1.00006pt}C^{\pm}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{\ell}\,,

(73)

with
C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)
being complex-analytic functions such that

[C^{+}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)]^{*}=C^{-}\hskip-1.00006pt(\bar{\xi})
and

|C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(0)|=1
.
The closure condition is guaranteed by
[d,\pounds_{y}]=0
,
provided that
d({\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{x}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{x}^{n}})=0
.
Note that we
leave
a possibility for

C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(0)

to carry a phase:

C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(0)={\mathrm{e}}^{\mp ih\varphi}
.
As argued
in
[
82
]
,

\omega^{\prime\pm}\mapsto{\mathrm{e}}^{\mp ih\varphi}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime\pm}

describes the electric-magnetic duality rotation
for helicity-
h
fields
if certain conditions are met.

Wilson coefficients on complex worldlines

The minimal coupling (
34
) is described by
the usual analytic continuation,

C^{+}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)=C^{-}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)={\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}&\,=\,{\mathrm{e}}^{+i\pounds_{y}}\,[\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{x}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{x}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,,\\
{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 1.00006pt{d{{\bar{z}}}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{{\bar{z}}}^{n}}&\,=\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\pounds_{y}}\,[\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{x}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{x}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,.\end{split}

(74)

Now we redefine the Wilson coefficients with respect to the minimal coupling as

\displaystyle C^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)\hskip 1.00006pt=:\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}\cdot f^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)

\displaystyle\,,\quad f^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt(\xi)=\textstyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}f^{\pm}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{\ell}\,.

(75)

Then (
73
) reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime+}&=f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(+i\pounds_{y})\,[\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,,\\
\omega^{\prime-}&=f^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt(-i\pounds_{y})\,[\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\hskip 1.00006pt{d{{\bar{z}}}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{{\bar{z}}}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt]\,.\end{split}

(76)

An intuitive
zig-zag
picture for (
76
) will be
a set of “distributed insertions” on the ribbon
with all types of zig-zag components.

Non-minimal symplectic perturbation

Direct computation shows that

\displaystyle f(i\pounds_{y})\hskip 1.00006pt\big({{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}}\big)

(77)

\displaystyle=\left({{\begin{array}[]{rl}&\big({f\hskip-0.50003pt(iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}\\
+&\big({f^{\prime}\hskip-0.50003pt(iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pti\hskip 1.00006pt{d{y}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}\\
+&\big({f^{\prime\prime}\hskip-0.50003pt(iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{i^{2}}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{y}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{y}^{n}}\end{array}}}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)=\left({{\begin{array}[]{rl}&\big({{{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}}[f](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}\\
+&\big({{{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{{\bar{z}}}^{n}}\\
+&\big({{{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}}[f](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)}\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{d{{\bar{z}}}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{{\bar{z}}}^{n}}\end{array}}}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)\,,

where by
y{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial
we mean
\pounds_{y}
acting on scalar functions.
This computation holds regardless of
the closure of
{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt{d{z}^{m}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{n}}
.
We have defined the polynomials

\displaystyle{{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi):=(1+(d/d\xi)+{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\kern 0.15pt(d/d\xi)^{2})\,f\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)

\displaystyle\,,\quad{{}^{0+1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi):={{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)+{{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)\,,

\displaystyle{{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi):=(-{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\kern 0.15pt(d/d\xi)-{\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\kern 0.15pt(d/d\xi)^{2})\,f\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)

\displaystyle\,,\quad{{}^{1+2}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi):={{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)+{{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)\,,

(84)

\displaystyle{{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi):=({\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}\kern 0.15pt(d/d\xi)^{2})\,f\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)

\displaystyle\,,\quad{{}^{0+1}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)+{{}^{1+2}\mathfrak{S}}[f](\xi)=f(\xi)\,.

We emphasize that a Newman-Janis shift of a generic tensor field
must shift not only the components but also the accompanying basis vectors/one-forms.
The
dy
terms that the Lie derivative
\pounds_{y}
induces
are crucial for
a) the closure of (
77
),
b) incorporating time-varying spin,
and
c) producing the correct
“zig-zag vertices” in
our
holomorphic/anti-holomorphic
perturbation theory.
Yet, not much attention has been paid to this point
in the literature.

From
(
77
),
we conclude that (
76
) matches with (
31a
) and (
31b
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{}^{0}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}={{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{+}](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\,,\quad{{}^{0}\omega^{\prime-}_{mn}}={{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{-}](-iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\,,\\
{{}^{1}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}={{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{+}](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\,,\quad{{}^{1}\omega^{\prime-}_{mn}}={{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{-}](-iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\,,\\
{{}^{2}\omega^{\prime+}_{mn}}={{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{+}](iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{+}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\,,\quad{{}^{2}\omega^{\prime-}_{mn}}={{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}}[f^{-}](-iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{-}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})\,.\end{split}

(85)

Plugging in (
85
) to (
65
)-(
66
),
one obtains the generic non-minimal heavenly equation of motion that incorporates all the single-curvature Wilson coefficients.
It is easy to check that

f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)=f^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)=1

derives (
53
) and (
55
),
while

f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)=f^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)={\mathrm{e}}^{-\xi}

derives (
63
).
The “interpolation” (
67
)
is described by

f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)=f^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)={\mathrm{e}}^{(g/2-1)\xi}
.

3.3
Impulse and classical-spin amplitudes

Finally, in this last subsection, we obtain
classical-spin
scattering amplitudes from
Born approximation in a monochromatic plane wave background

\displaystyle\phi_{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}(z)=(i\nu/2)\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\,,

(86)

which
is interpreted as describing
a positive-frequency field quantum carrying positive helicity
[
172
,
173
]
.
Geometrically,
the spinor-helicity variables

{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}
and
o_{\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}[{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}]^{*}

of the field quanta
introduce a principal null direction to spacetime.
Accordingly, we install a Newman-Penrose spin frame
[
118
]

\langle\iota o\rangle=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}o_{\alpha}\iota_{\beta}=1={\bar{\epsilon}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{\iota}}_{\dot{\beta}}={\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{o}}{\bar{\iota}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}
.
Both the “relativist” and “amplitudes” notations will be used interchangeably:

o_{\alpha}=:{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}
,
{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}=:{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}}
.

Complexified 3pt on-shell kinematics

As a preliminary, we introduce a one-parameter family of complexified on-shell kinematics
for equal-mass massive-massive-massless amplitudes.
This is relevant to our discussion because
the massive ambitwistor description computes
not only the momentum impulse
\mathit{\Delta}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}
but also
\mathit{\Delta}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
and
\mathit{\Delta}{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
separately.
Suppose
a particle of fixed mass
m
gets deflected by absorbing a massless momentum
-{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}}
:

\displaystyle|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}|=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}|+|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\,,\quad|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle[{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}]=mx|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\,.

(87)

The
x
-factor encodes the equal-mass condition.
The real kinematics is given by (e.g.,
[
7
]
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle&{}=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle+(x/2m)\hskip 1.00006pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\,,\quad{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}|{}={\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}|+(1/2mx)\hskip 1.00006pt{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\,,\end{split}

(88)

which is the unique solution to (
87
)
that satisfies the reality condition
[{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}]^{*}={\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
,
[{\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}]^{*}

={\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
.
But, there is a one-parameter family of solutions
if we drop the reality condition:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle&{}=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle+(1-\vartheta/2)\hskip 0.50003pt(x/2m)\hskip 1.00006pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\,,\\
{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}|&{}={\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}|+(1+\vartheta/2)\hskip 0.50003pt(1/2mx)\hskip 1.00006pt{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\,.\end{split}

(89)

The parameter
\vartheta
controls the ratio between the contributions from
\mathit{\Delta}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
and
\mathit{\Delta}{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}

that add up to
\mathit{\Delta}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}=-{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}}
.
As depicted in the left side of Figure
2
,
the equation
|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle+{t\hskip 1.00006pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle}
has a geometric interpretation as a null rotation
[
89
]
on the celestial sphere
that takes the flagpole direction of the spinor
|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle
as the fixed point.
Then (
89
) is a complexified Lorentz transformation that
null rotates the celestial spheres of the right-handed and left-handed spinors differently.

In analogy with
the MHV kinematics

[
174
]
, we consider the following two extreme cases.

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leftrightarrow\,\,\,\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rl}|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle&{}=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\\
{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}|&{}={\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}|+(1/mx)\hskip 1.00006pt{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\end{array}}\right.

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leftrightarrow\,\,\,\left\{{\begin{array}[]{rl}|{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle&{}=|{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle+(x/m)\hskip 1.00006pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\\
{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}|&{}={\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}|\end{array}}\right.

These complexified kinematics
naturally arise from the minimally coupled heavenly equations of motion;
for instance, (
53
) parallel-transports
\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
while preserving
{\bar{\Delta}}
.
They are associated with zig-zag vector fields
(as operators on the
(\lambda,{\bar{\lambda}})
Lagrange submanifold, say)
as the following:

\displaystyle\includegraphics[scale,valign]{figs/hkin-.pdf}\,\,\,\sim\,\,\,\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v-.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD.pdf}\,,\qquad\quad\includegraphics[scale,valign]{figs/hkin+.pdf}\,\,\,\sim\,\,\,\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}\,.

(91)

Minimal impulse and amplitude

The amplitudes
can be conveniently obtained by
computing the interaction action for a zeroth-order trajectory in an on-shell plane wave background (cf. Appendix A of
[
10
]
).
However, to keep pursuing the “symplectic form only” philosophy, we rather relate the zig-zag expansions to amplitudes by computing the impulses.
The zig-zag vector field (
51
) is evaluated for (
86
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\smash{\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}}\,\hskip 1.00006pt&=\,\,\{{\,\,\,\,\,},z^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\}^{\circ}\,\hskip 1.00006pt(2/\Delta)\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}(z)\,,\\
&=\,\,\{{\,\,\,\,\,},z^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\}^{\circ}\,\hskip 1.00006pti\big(\hskip 1.00006pt\nu\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}{\kern 0.09995pt}{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\beta}}{\bar{o}}_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}}\vphantom{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}/\Delta\,,\\
&=\,\,\{{\,\,\,\,\,},z^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\}^{\circ}\,\hskip 1.00006pti\big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\nu\langle\iota|p|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\bar{o}}yo}\hskip 1.00006pto_{\alpha}{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}-\nu\langle o|p|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\bar{o}}yo}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\alpha}{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i{\bar{o}}xo}/\Delta\,.\end{split}

(92)

We have used
\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}=o_{\alpha}\iota^{\beta}-\iota_{\alpha}o^{\beta}
in the last line.
The heavenly “ambitwistor impulse” follows from
integrating (
92
) over time:
9
9

9

One could argue that
we should rather
not straightforwardly integrate (
92
) but
evaluate the impulse on the space of
“boundary” data.
Yet, this caveat does not affect
the constant-mass spinorial frame impulse we are considering here (and
\mathit{\Delta}y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
as well).
See (
122
)
for a more accurate treatment.

\displaystyle\mathit{\Delta}Z_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}=0\,,\quad\mathit{\Delta}{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{d\tau}{-2}\,\Big\langle d{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}},-\smash{\includegraphics[valign]{figs/l-b.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/v+.pdf}\includegraphics[valign]{figs/xD-b.pdf}}\Big\rangle\,.

(93)

The recipe to obtain the amplitude is
identifying
the amount of impulse expected from the particle interpretation (absorption of a single quantum)
and then
reading off the proportionality factor from
\mathit{\Delta}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}
.
In the first-order approximation,
the integration
(
93
)
does nothing other than inducing the delta function
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(\langle o|{\mathsf{1}}|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt)
.
Thus, we can just directly read off the “amplitude times momentum kick”

(\nu m_{0}x\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a})(-{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}})

from the bracketed term in the last line of (
92
)
by putting
\langle o|p|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}=0

and plugging in the scattering data

p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}{\,=\,}{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}
,
y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\,=\,}{{-}a^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}}
.
In this sense, the vector field (
92
) is a “symplectic avatar” of the positive-helicity three-point amplitude.
Note
that it performs a null rotation on
{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
,
y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
:

\displaystyle\{{\,\,\,\,\,},z^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\}^{\circ}\,\hskip 1.00006pti\nu\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\alpha{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}}{\kern 0.09995pt}{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\beta}}{\bar{o}}_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}}\vphantom{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}}{\Delta}\,=\,\bigg(\hskip 0.50003pt{{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}-y^{{\dot{\alpha}}\beta}\hskip-0.50003pt\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}}\right|_{z}\hskip 1.00006pt}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pti\nu\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\beta}}{\bar{o}}_{\smash{{\dot{\alpha}}}\vphantom{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}}{\Delta}\,.

(94)

The amplitude
\nu m_{0}x\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}
is precisely the scalar amplitude
e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}

[
3
]

times the minimal exponential spin factor
[
8
,
7
]

if

\displaystyle\nu=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptx^{h-1}\,,

(95)

where

collectively denotes the coupling constants of Yang-Mills (
h{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}1
) and gravity (
h{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}2
).
We revisit this finding later in section
4.1
.

Figure 2:

Null rotation on the left and right skies.
As a conformal transformation
on the celestial sphere, it preserves the angle that
the null flag of the spinor being transformed
makes with the circular flow lines.
This provides a geometrical way of seeing

\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{2}}^{I}\rangle=\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle

or

[{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}]=[{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}]

[

]
.

Generic impulse and amplitude

For non-minimal couplings,
the interaction with the self-dual plane wave
alters both
\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
and
{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
.
Consider the maximally non-minimal coupling for an example;
the first two “avatar” vector fields (
60a
)-(
60b
) contribute to the first-order
\mathit{\Delta}{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
,
while (

)-(

) contribute to
\mathit{\Delta}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
.
Generalizing, one finds

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\lambda}}_{I}|&\hskip 1.00006pt=\,i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,{\cdot}\,{{}^{0+1}\mathfrak{S}[f^{+}]({\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{m_{0}x}}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\,,\\
\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}|\lambda^{I}\rangle&\hskip 1.00006pt=\,i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,{\cdot}\,{{}^{1+2}\mathfrak{S}[f^{+}]({\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,{\textstyle\frac{x}{m_{0}}}\hskip 0.50003pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\,,\end{split}

(96)

while replacing
iy^{m}\partial/\partial x^{m}
with

i(-a^{m})(i{\mathsf{3}}_{m})={\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a
.
The
x
-factor is defined on the support of
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathsf{1}})
.
Adding up these two
and using the identity

{{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}[f](\xi)}+2\hskip 1.00006pt{{}^{1}\mathfrak{S}[f](\xi)}+{{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}[f](\xi)}=f(\xi)
, we find the momentum impulse

\displaystyle\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}=i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,{\cdot}\,f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,(-{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}})\,.

(97)

For concreteness, we also spell out the negative-helicity counterparts.
A positive-frequency field quantum carrying negative helicity is described by

{\bar{\phi}}_{\alpha\beta}\hskip-0.50003pt({\bar{z}})=(i\nu/2)\hskip 1.00006pto_{\alpha}o_{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i{\bar{o}}{\bar{z}}o}

(note that it is not the straightforward complex conjugate of (
86
)).
We find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\lambda}}_{I}|&\hskip 1.00006pt=\,i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x^{-1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 0.81949pt\cdot\hskip 0.81949pt}a}\,{\cdot}\,{{}^{1+2}\mathfrak{S}[f^{-}](-{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{m_{0}x}}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}_{I}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|\,,\\
\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}|\lambda^{I}\rangle&\hskip 1.00006pt=\,i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x^{-1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 0.81949pt\cdot\hskip 0.81949pt}a}\,{\cdot}\,{{}^{0+1}\mathfrak{S}[f^{-}](-{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,{\textstyle\frac{x}{m_{0}}}\hskip 0.50003pt|{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\,,\end{split}

(98)

\displaystyle\mathit{\Delta}^{(1)}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 1.00006pt=\,i\Big(\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\nu m_{0}x^{-1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 0.81949pt\cdot\hskip 0.81949pt}a}\,{\cdot}\,f^{-}\hskip-0.50003pt(-{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,(-{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}_{\dot{\alpha}})\,.

(99)

The helicity flip is effectively implemented by the sign change of the pseudovector
a^{m}
.

To summarize, we have obtained the minimal and non-minimal amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathrm{e}}^{\pm{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,,\quad{\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt[f^{\pm}]({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})={\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})\,{\cdot}\,f^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\pm{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)\,.

(100)

For example, the “exponential” arbitrary-
g
coupling (
67
) leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)={\mathrm{e}}^{(g/2-1)\xi}&{\quad\implies\quad}{\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt[f^{\pm}]({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})={\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})\,{\cdot}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{\pm(g/2-1)\hskip 0.40974pt{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.16391pt\cdot\kern 0.16391pt}a}\,.\end{split}

(101)

Moreover, we also derived classical complexified kinematics corresponding to the non-minimal amplitudes.
Peeling off the amplitudes
{\mathcal{M}}^{\pm h}\hskip-0.50003pt[f^{\pm}]({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})

from the impulses (
96
) and (
98
),
we find that
the “asymmetry parameter”
\vartheta
of (
89
) is given by

\displaystyle\pm\vartheta\,=\,{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{{}^{0-2}\mathfrak{S}[f^{\pm}](\pm{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}\hskip 1.00006pt:=\hskip 1.00006pt{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{{}^{0}\mathfrak{S}[f^{\pm}](\pm{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}-{{}^{2}\mathfrak{S}[f^{\pm}](\pm{\mathsf{3}}{\kern 0.20004pt\cdot\kern 0.20004pt}a)}}\big)

(102)

for positive-helicity and negative-helicity amplitudes, respectively.
It is easy to see that (
67
) leads to
\vartheta=g
.
Especially,

leads to the real kinematics (
88
).

Matching with definite-spin amplitudes

Finally, we explicate the connection with the discrete definite-spin amplitudes.
Previous studies
[

,
2
,
8
,
11
,
21
,
10
]

have established a dictionary between the Wilson coefficients
C^{\pm}_{\ell}
and the
coupling constants
g^{\pm}_{\ell}
appearing in the generic three-point amplitude
[
3
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{+h}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt[g^{+}]({\mathsf{2}}{\leftarrow}{\mathsf{1}};{\mathsf{3}})

\displaystyle\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pte_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{2s}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{1}}\rangle^{\kern-0.40974pt\odot(2s-\ell)}{\hskip 1.00006pt\odot}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\hskip-0.50003pt\frac{x}{m_{0}/\hbar}\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{3}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{3}}{\mathsf{1}}\rangle\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\kern-2.25342pt\odot\ell}\,,

(103a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{-h}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt[g^{-}]({\mathsf{2}}{\leftarrow}{\mathsf{1}};{\mathsf{3}})

\displaystyle\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pte_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{2s}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{-}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}^{-1}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}^{\kern-0.40974pt\odot(2s-\ell)}{\hskip 1.00006pt\odot}\hskip-1.00006pt\left(\hskip-0.50003pt\frac{1}{(m_{0}/\hbar)x}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}^{-1}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\hskip-1.00006pt\right)^{\kern-2.25342pt\odot\ell}\,.

(103b)

We have temporarily restored
\hbar
.
10
10

10

Our convention for the
\hbar{\,\neq\,}0
unit is such that spinor-helicity variables have the dimension of
(\text{length})^{-1/2}

so that they decompose massive/massless wavenumbers
{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}},{\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}},{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\sim(\text{length})^{-1}
.
The mu variables have the dimension of
(\text{length})^{1/2}
,
and the symplectic form reads

\omega^{\circ}=i\hbar\,d{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dZ_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}
.

We restrict our attention to the diagonal (equal-mass and equal-spin) sector.
As a result of the matching, one finds

\displaystyle{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}{\,\cdot\,}f^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006ptC^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}{\,\cdot\,}\hskip 0.50003ptg^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(-\xi/|\vec{W}|){\quad\implies\quad}f^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006ptg^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(-\xi/|\vec{W}|)\,,

(104)

where the generating function describing the classical limit of
g^{\pm}_{\ell}
is defined as

\displaystyle g^{\pm}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi):=\lim_{\hbar\to 0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}g^{\pm}_{\ell}(\hbar\xi)^{\ell}\,.

(105)

Notice that Wilson coefficients on complex worldlines,
f^{\pm}_{\ell}
,
are more straightforwardly related to the coupling constants
g^{\pm}_{\ell}
.
As a pedagogical demonstration,
we re-derive (
104
)
in appendix
B

without resorting to the “real-based” Wilson coefficients
C^{\pm}_{\ell}
.
We briefly discuss how the spin coherent states are implemented in the spherical top framework and then reproduce
[
10
]
’s matching calculation with our notations and conventions.
We also observe that using the complexified kinematics (
90
)-(
90
),
rather than the real kinematics (
88
),
provides a “shortcut”
for the calculations.

4
Scattering of
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr in Heavenly Plane Waves

In the previous section, we have studied
“universal” implications of relating chirality and holomorphy
on the linear-order perturbative physics,
leaving
\phi

unspecified.
In this section,
we
finally
specialize
\phi
to Yang-Mills and gravity.
Still, we
continue pursuing a “bottom-up” approach:
we
reverse engineer the
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr actions
in heavenly backgrounds
from the matching (
95
) between the minimal amplitude
e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}
and the plane wave ansatz (
86
),
without having a top-down picture.
Furthermore,
we also exactly solve the classical dynamics of
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr in
self-dual plane-wave backgrounds of arbitrarily (non-perturbatively) large magnitudes
and re-derive their impulses as exact quantities,
thus examining the validity of the results of Section
3.3

for
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr.

4.1
Corrections from symplectivity

Heavenly plane-wave symplectic perturbations

The classical-spin amplitudes
of
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr particles
are given as
[
7
,
10
]

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{+h}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptmx^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,,\quad(e_{1},e_{2})=(\sqrt{2}g\hskip 1.00006ptq_{a}c^{a},m{\hskip-0.24994pt/\hskip-0.50003pt}{M_{\text{Pl}}})\,,

(106)

where

g
and
M_{\text{Pl}}:=(8\pi G)^{-1/2}
are the Yang-Mills coupling constant and the Planck mass, respectively.

q_{a}
denotes the non-abelian color charge of the
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
particle,
and
c^{a}
denotes the color “polarization.”
As already pointed out in (
95
),
the amplitude
\nu mx\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}

boils down to (
106
) if

\nu=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptx^{h-1}

on the support of
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathsf{1}})
.
A natural guess is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{{\text{$\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}$}}}:\quad\nu=\sqrt{2}g\hskip 1.00006ptq_{a}c^{a}\,,\qquad\text{Kerr}:\quad\nu={M_{\text{Pl}}}^{-1}(-\iota^{\alpha}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\alpha}})\,.\end{split}

(107)

Accordingly,
we can deduce that the
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}
and Kerr particles have the following symplectic perturbations in
the
background (
86
):
11
11

11

The right-hand sides of (
108a
)-(
108b
) apparently have mass dimension
[M^{-1}]

because we make the mode operator (which carries
[M^{1}]
) implicit.
One can assign this “hidden
[M^{1}]
” to
{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}
,
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathsf{1}})
,
etc.

\displaystyle\text{{\text{$\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}$}}}:\quad\omega^{\prime+}

\displaystyle=q_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{ig\hskip 1.00006ptc^{a}}{\sqrt{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt(dz{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz)^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}+(\cdots)\,,

(108a)

\displaystyle\text{Kerr}:\quad\omega^{\prime+}

\displaystyle=p_{{\gamma}{\dot{{\gamma}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{-i\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}^{\dot{{\gamma}}}\iota^{\gamma}}{2{M_{\text{Pl}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt(dz{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz)^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}+(\cdots)\,.

(108b)

We have denoted
(dz{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz)^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}:=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}({d{z}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\hskip-2.10007pt\wedge\hskip-0.50003ptd{z}^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}})
.

Closure condition

The astute reader may have noticed that,
while we have worked as if
\nu
is a constant in previous sections,

q_{a}c^{a}
and
(-\iota^{\alpha}p_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\alpha}})
are actually not.
Instead, they turn out to be constants of motion:
consider how
d\nu{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{o}}|dz|\iota\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}
contributes to the zig-zag expansion of
\{\nu,{\bar{\Delta}}\}
.
But still, they are not constants “off-shell,”
so
additional non-
dz{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz
type terms are needed for
the closure

d\omega^{\prime+}=0

as indicated already as ellipses in (
108
).
Noting that the auxiliary spinor
\iota_{\alpha}

can be traded off with derivatives as

\displaystyle\begin{split}i\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\big({\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\iota^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\big)&={\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\,,\\
i\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{{\gamma}{\dot{{\gamma}}}}\big(i\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{{\delta}{\dot{{\delta}}}}\big({\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt\iota^{\gamma}\hskip 0.50003pt\iota^{\delta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\big)\big)&=i\hskip 0.50003pt\partial_{{\delta}{\dot{{\delta}}}}\big({\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{{\gamma}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\iota^{\delta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\big)={\bar{o}}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{{\gamma}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\dot{{\delta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\,,\end{split}

(109)

we can complete (
108a
)-(
108b
) as
(notice a double copy structure)

\displaystyle\text{{\text{$\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}$}}}:\quad\omega^{\prime+}

\displaystyle=d\Big(\hskip 1.00006ptg\hskip 1.00006ptq_{a}c^{a}(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\smash{{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}})\hskip 1.00006ptdz^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,,

(110a)

\displaystyle\text{Kerr}:\quad\omega^{\prime+}

\displaystyle=d\Big(\hskip 0.50003pt{\textstyle-\frac{1}{2M_{\text{Pl}}}}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota^{\alpha}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}^{\dot{\alpha}})(\hskip-1.00006pt\sqrt{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{o}}_{\smash{{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{\beta}})\hskip 1.00006ptdz^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.40974pt{\bar{o}zo}}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big)\,.

(110b)

We see that the additional terms have the forms
dq{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz
and
dp{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dz
, respectively.
Both are admittable
in the standard of (
26
).

Thankfully,
since
