Appendix A
TBMT and MPTD equations

TBMT

The Thomas-Bargmann-Michel-Telegdi equation

[
41
,
42
,
43
,
44
,
45
,
46
]

describes a spinning particle moving in a background electromagnetic field
in special relativity.
In this paper,
by a massive spinning particle is governed by the TBMT equation with a certain
g
value
we mean that its momentum and spin length pseudovector
obey the following equations
in the covariant spin gauge with the gauge fixing
-p_{m}\hskip 0.50003pty^{m}=0
of the residual redundancy:

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{p}_{m}+p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{n}{}_{m}/m+\mathcal{O}(y^{1})\,,\\
0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{y}^{m}-\frac{1}{m}\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{\frac{g}{2}\,\phi^{m}{}_{n}-\Big(1{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}\frac{g}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big)\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,\phi^{l}{}_{n}}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}+\mathcal{O}(y^{2})\,.\end{split}

(181)

\phi_{mn}
is an antisymmetric tensor.
We use these terminologies
“TBMT equation” and “
g
factor”
in a broad, general sense
that does not restrict

\phi_{mn}

to be the field strength of electromagnetism.
For instance,
it can be something depending on other dynamical variables of the spinning particle
such as
q_{a}F^{a}{}_{mn}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
of Yang-Mills
or
2\hskip 0.50003ptp_{r}\gamma^{r}{}_{[mn]}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
of gravity.

MPTD

The Mathisson-Papapetrou-Tulczyjew-Dixon equation
[
47
,
48
,
49
,
50
,
51
,
52
,
53
,
54
]

describes
the dynamics of
a spinning particle in curved spacetime.
It is extended by a parameter
\kappa
,
which is
called the “gravimagnetic ratio”
in the literature

[
55
,
56
,
57
,
58
,
59
,
60
,
61
]
.
Our version
reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&{}\,=\,{}m\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\mu}-p^{m}+\mathcal{O}(y^{1})\,,\\
0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{p}_{m}-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{n}{}_{ms}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}-\frac{g}{2}\,p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star R}^{n}{}_{mrs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{r}\hskip 0.50003pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}(y^{2})\,,\\
0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{y}^{m}+\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{\frac{g}{2}\,\gamma^{m}{}_{ns}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)-\Big(1{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}\frac{g}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big)\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,\gamma^{l}{}_{ns}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\hskip 1.00006pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\\
&{}\phantom{{}\,=\,{}\dot{y}^{m}}{}+\bigg(\hskip 1.00006pt{\kappa\,{\star R}^{m}{}_{nrs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)-\Big(\hskip 0.50003pt\frac{g}{2}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}\kappa\Big)\,\frac{\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{m}p_{l}}\hskip 0.24994pt}{m^{2}}\,{\star R}^{l}{}_{nrs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)}\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\hskip 1.00006pty^{r}\hskip 1.00006pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}(y^{3})\,,\end{split}

(182)

with the covariant gauge and
the constraint

-p_{m}\hskip 0.50003pty^{m}=0
.

e^{m}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
,

\gamma^{m}{}_{ns}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)
,

R^{m}{}_{nrs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)

denote the
tetrad,
spin connection coefficients,
and Riemann tensor.
As in (
181
),
the momentum equation
sets
the “zero point”
of non-minimal parameters:
the precessions of
p_{m}
and
y^{m}
are synchronized when

1=g/2=\kappa
.
By including
g
in (
182
), we are being maximally pedantic,
intending to maximally mimic
the amplitude.
Of course, it is
fixed
to
g{\,=\,}2
by
the general covariance of the spin vector:
the spin vector should behave as the same as
two infinitesimally deviated points.
For more details,
see Appendix B of
[
9
]
.

Either of the two bracketed terms in the
\dot{y}^{m}
equation
can be viewed as a “double copy” of the bracketed term in (
181
).
This is because there exist two ways of drawing analogies between electromagnetism/Yang-Mills and gravity.
The first term,
which defines the “gravitational gyromagnetic ratio”
g
,
follows from (
181
) by
taking

\phi_{mn}=-m\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{mn\rho}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\rho}\,(\sim p_{r}\hskip 0.50003pt2\gamma^{r}{}_{[mn]}\hskip-0.50003pt(x))
.
Then one is comparing
A
to
e^{m}
and
F
to
\gamma^{m}{}_{n}

as in Kaluza-Klein reduction
[
196
,
197
,
198
,
199
,
200
]
or the gravitoelectromagnetism formulation of linearized gravity
[
201
,
202
,
203
,
204
,
205
,
206
,
207
,
208
,
209
,
210
,
211
,
212
,
213
,
214
,
215
,
216
,
217
,
218
]
,
which realizes the “connection as field strength” argument in
[
82
]
.
This parallel is suggested from
the usual formulation of flat-space graviton scattering amplitudes in terms of metric perturbations
and also from
the “exact sequences of electromagnetism and gravity” diagram in
[
82
]
.
More poetically,
we could say
“Aharanov-Bohm double copies to Sagnac”
[
219
]
:
the Coriolis force is the gravitomagnetic force.
On the other hand, the second term follows from (
181
) by
taking

\phi_{mn}=-m\,{\star R}_{mnrs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{r}\hskip 0.50003pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}

while
putting
g{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}2

and replacing
g/2
with
\kappa
.
Then
one is comparing
A
to
\gamma^{m}{}_{n}
and
F
to
R^{m}{}_{n}
.
This way of thinking is more natural in a background independent/non-perturbative set-up
and is encouraged by
Ashtekar variables
[
220
,
221
,
222
,
223
,
224
,
225
,
226
]
,
non-linear graviton
[
157
,
156
]
, Weyl double copy
[
227
,
228
,
229
,
230
]
,
covariant color-kinematics duality
[
231
]
,
etc.

Both of these two parallels
emerge in our discussion on
the
symplectic perturbation theory
of spinning particles:
the former from the non-covariant scheme
employed in this paper
and the latter from the covariant scheme
employed in the follow-up paper
[
83
]
.

Generic
\mathcal{O}(\phi^{1})
spin precession equation of motion

In this paper, we have proposed a generic spin precession equation of motion, all orders in spin and first order in the formal “field strength”
\phi
.
When
\phi
is self-dual, it reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{p}_{m}+\frac{1}{m}\,p_{n}\Bigg[\hskip 1.00006pt{\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{(C_{\ell}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\tilde{C}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\star^{-1})}{\ell!}\hskip 1.00006pt\big({(y{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)^{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star^{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\phi}(x)}\big)\hskip-0.24994pt{}^{n}{}_{m}}\Bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})\,,\\
0&{}\,=\,{}\dot{y}^{m}-\frac{1}{m}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\left[{\hskip-1.00006pt\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{(C_{\ell+1}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\tilde{C}_{\ell+1}\hskip 1.00006pt\star^{-1})}{\ell!}\hskip 1.00006pt\big({(y{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)^{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star^{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\phi}(x)}\big)\hskip-0.24994pt{}^{m}{}_{n}\\
\displaystyle{}-\frac{p^{m}p_{r}}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{(C_{\ell}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\tilde{C}_{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\star^{-1})-(C_{\ell+1}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\tilde{C}_{\ell+1}\hskip 1.00006pt\star^{-1})}{\ell!}\hskip 1.00006pt\big({(y{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)^{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star^{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\phi}(x)}\big)\hskip-0.24994pt{}^{r}{}_{n}\end{array}\hskip-0.24994pt\hskip-0.50003pt\hskip-1.00006pt}\right]\hskip-0.50003pty^{n}\\
&\quad\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})\,,\end{split}

(183)

where
we have also included magnetic Wilson coefficients

\tilde{C}_{\ell}

for full generality.
One obtains
(
181
) and (
182
)
by truncating (
183
)
with having

C_{1}{\,=\,}g/2
,
C_{2}{\,=\,}\kappa
,
and
\tilde{C}_{\ell}{\,=\,}0
.

As pointed out recently in
[
2
]
,

\dot{p}_{m}
and
\dot{y}^{m}

can be described together in a more unified manner
in terms of the spin frames
as
\dot{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{I}
,
\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}{}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}
.
16
16

16

This idea can be traced back to
the works
[
123
,
232
,
233
,
234
]
.

This is best illustrated
if
the body-frame components
of the spin
pseudovector
are constant,
which happens in our setting
when
the particle has a constant Regge trajectory.
The spin frame version of (
183
) reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}{}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}&{}\,=\,{}{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{I{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Bigg[\hskip 1.00006pt{\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{\hskip 0.24994pt\ell!\hskip 0.24994pt}\frac{C^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-1.00006pt{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}C^{+}_{\ell+1}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt(iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)^{\ell}\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)}\Bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})\,,\\
m_{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{I}&{}\,=\,{}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{J}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{J{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\Bigg[\hskip 1.00006pt{\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{\hskip 0.24994pt\ell!\hskip 0.24994pt}\frac{C^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-1.00006pt{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}C^{+}_{\ell+1}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt(iy{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\partial)^{\ell}\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)}\Bigg]({\bar{\lambda}}^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})\,,\end{split}

(184)

where
C^{\pm}_{\ell}=C_{\ell}{\,\mp\,}i\hskip 0.50003pt\tilde{C}_{\ell}
.
One can clearly see that

\dot{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{I}=0+\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})

in self-dual backgrounds
implies
C^{+}_{\ell}{\,=\,}C^{+}_{0}{\,=\,}1

and vice versa:

all
three-point spin multipole couplings
(not only dipole and quadrupole, to emphasize)
to the self-dual field
are Kerr-Newman.
17
17

17

We clarify that
by “Kerr-Newman coupling”
we mean
C^{\pm}_{\ell}{\,=\,}1

for both helicities
1
and
2
.
The electromagnetic coupling of the Kerr-Newman black hole coincides with
the
\sqrt{\text{Kerr\hskip 0.50003pt}}

[

]
.

), (

for reference.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}{}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}&{}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\textstyle\frac{1+g/2}{2}\,\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{I{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt\phi^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{\dot{\alpha}}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)/m_{0}+\mathcal{O}(y^{1})\,,\\

(185)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{{\bar{\lambda}}}{}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}&{}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\textstyle-{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{I{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big[\hskip 0.50003pt{\frac{1+g/2}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt\gamma^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}s}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)+\frac{g/2+\kappa}{2}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}rs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{r}+\mathcal{O}(y^{2})}\hskip 0.24994pt\Big]\,e^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\\[-0.86108pt]
\dot{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{I}&{}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt\textstyle-\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{J}\,{\bar{\lambda}}_{\smash{J{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big[\hskip 0.50003pt{\frac{1-g/2}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt\gamma^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}s}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)+\frac{g/2-\kappa}{2}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\dot{\beta}}{}_{{\dot{\alpha}}rs}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{r}+\mathcal{O}(y^{2})}\hskip 0.24994pt\Big]({\bar{\lambda}}^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}\,e^{s}{}_{\sigma}\hskip-0.50003pt(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\end{split}

(186)

Appendix B
Matching Calculation

Classical spin from coherent states

Aoude and Ochirov

[
10
]

have
explicated the procedure of obtaining massive spinning amplitudes in the classical spin limit
in terms of spin coherent states.
We briefly review their methodology in our notation.
See also
[
17
]
for a succinct review.

Modeling the spinning degrees of freedom
by the coadjoint orbit
[
235
,
236
,
237
,
238
,
239
]
,
the classical value of spin angular momentum is identified with its coherent-state expectation value in the first-quantized theory as

\displaystyle W_{a}=(\hbar/2)\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{I}(\sigma_{a})_{I}{}^{J}\psi_{J}\,,\quad|\vec{W}|=(\hbar/2)\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{I}\psi_{I}\,.

(187)

The classical-spin amplitude then follows from the
coherent-state
expectation value of all definite-spin amplitudes in the
\hbar\to 0
limit with
|\vec{W}|
fixed:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}}){\phantom{{}:={}}\mathllap{{}={}}}{}&\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hbar\to 0\\
{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi=2|\vec{W}|/\hbar\end{subarray}}\sum_{2s=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}},\psi;{\mathsf{3}})\,,\\
\mathcal{M}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}},\psi;{\mathsf{3}}):={}&\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}}{(2s)!}\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{J_{1}}\cdots{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{J_{2s}}\hskip 1.00006pt(\mathcal{A}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{2}}{\leftarrow}{\mathsf{1}};{\mathsf{3}}))_{J_{1}\cdots J_{2s}}{}^{I_{1}\cdots I_{2s}}\,\psi_{I_{1}}\cdots\psi_{I_{2s}}\,,\end{split}

(188)

where
{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}
is the measure normalizing coherent states.
For example, the spin-tensor
{\hbox to0.0pt{1\hss}\hskip 1.6pt\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{1.1}[1.1]{{\hbox{{1}}}}}}^{\hskip-0.81949pt\odot 2s}
of the plus-helicity minimal amplitude

\smash{\mathcal{A}^{+h}_{(2s)}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{2}}{\leftarrow}{\mathsf{1}};{\mathsf{3}})=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\hbox to0.0pt{1\hss}\hskip 1.6pt\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{1.1}[1.1]{{\hbox{{1}}}}}}^{\hskip-0.81949pt\odot 2s}}

boils down to

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\Big({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{J}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}_{J}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\psi_{I}\hskip-1.00006pt\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,,\end{split}

(189)

upon plugging in the real on-shell kinematics given in (
88
)

[
7
,
10
]
.
Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}_{J}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle&=\delta_{J}{}^{I}-{\textstyle\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt\langle{\mathsf{1}}^{-1}_{I}{\mathsf{3}}\rangle{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}^{-1}{}^{J}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\,,\\
&=\delta_{J}{}^{I}+{\mathsf{3}}_{m}\Big(\hskip-0.50003pt{\textstyle\frac{\hbar}{2m_{0}}\hskip 0.50003pt(\Lambda({\mathsf{1}}))^{m}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003pt(\sigma^{a})_{I}{}^{J}+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}}^{-1})^{m}\hskip 0.50003pt\delta_{I}{}^{J}}\kern-0.20004pt\Big)\,,\\
&=\delta_{J}{}^{I}+{\textstyle\frac{\hbar}{2m_{0}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}_{m}(\Lambda({\mathsf{1}}))^{m}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003pt(\sigma^{a})_{I}{}^{J}\end{split}

(190)

on the support of
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{1}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathsf{3}})
.
In turn, it follows that

\displaystyle{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{J}\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}_{J}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\psi_{I}-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{I}\psi_{I}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a({\mathsf{1}})\,.

(191)

In our spherical top setting,
such classical-spin states
are realized in the
(\lambda,{\bar{\lambda}})
polarization as
polynomials of
\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
and
({\bar{\lambda}}^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}

saturated by a common spinor
\psi_{I}
;
i.e., they are the “right” coherent states with respect to the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
group action on
\mathbb{MA}
.
The unoccupied “left”
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
indices contract to amplitudes as spacetime spinor-tensors.

Matching

Applying (
188
) with the real kinematics (
88
),
the coherent-state expectation value of the plus-helicity spinning amplitude (
103a
)
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{=}\,\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pte_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{2s=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{2s}\frac{\hskip 0.50003ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip 0.50003pt}{(2s)!}\hskip 1.00006pt\big({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a\big)^{2s-\ell}(-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a)^{\ell}\,,\\
&=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\frac{-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}{\vphantom{\hbox{\raise 1.99997pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}$}}}}}}}{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}\right)^{\kern-1.39304pt\ell}\sum_{2s=\ell}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(2s)!}\hskip 0.50003pt\big({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a\big)^{2s}\,,\\
&=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\frac{-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}{\vphantom{\hbox{\raise 1.99997pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}$}}}}}}}{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}\right)^{\kern-1.39304pt\ell}\left(\hskip-0.50003pt1-\frac{1}{(\ell-1)!}\hskip 1.00006pt\Gamma\big(\ell\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a\big)\hskip-1.00006pt\kern-0.15pt\right)\,.\end{split}

(192)

As argued in
[
10
]
around equations (3.22)-(3.27),
for large
{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi
this has the limit

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{+h}\hskip-0.50003pt[g^{+}](x,a;{\mathsf{3}})=e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\lim_{\hbar\to 0}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg({-\frac{{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}{\hskip 0.50003pt|\vec{W}|/\hbar\hskip 0.50003pt}}\bigg)^{\kern-1.39304pt\ell}\,\right)\,.\end{split}

(193)

Therefore, provided that
g^{+}_{\ell}
scales as
\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{-\ell})
,
we conclude that

\displaystyle f^{+}_{\ell}=(-|\vec{W}|)^{-\ell}\cdot\lim_{\hbar\to 0}g^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}{\quad\implies\quad}f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(\xi)=g^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt(-\xi/|\vec{W}|)

(194)

by comparing (
193
) with

\mathcal{M}^{+h}\hskip-0.50003pt[f^{+}]({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})=\mathcal{M}^{+h}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})\,{\cdot}\,f^{+}\hskip-0.50003pt({\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a)

of (
100
).

A trick

Lastly, we demonstrate a slightly quicker derivation.
As shown in (
189
),
the real kinematics (
88
) has induced the minimal exponential spin factor
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}
.
However, we find that we can either apply the complexified kinematics (
90
) and put
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}
by hand.
For minimal coupling, it works as

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip-0.50003pt=\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}{\quad\implies\quad}{\mathcal{M}}({\mathsf{1}},a;{\mathsf{3}})\hskip 1.00006pt&=\hskip 1.00006pte_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pte^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\left({\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\Big({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}^{J}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\langle{\mathsf{2}}^{-1}_{J}{\mathsf{1}}^{I}\rangle\psi_{I}\hskip-1.00006pt\Big)\right)\,,\\
\hskip 1.00006pt&=\hskip 1.00006pte_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pte^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\,.\end{split}

(195)

This trick may be thought of as
an analog of
taking the holomorphic complex worldline as the “zero point”:
the
Newman-Janis shifted
amplitude
is taken as default.
One can check that it also works in the non-minimal case:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{=}\,\,\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\left(e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.16391pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{2s=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{2s}\frac{\hskip 0.50003ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip 0.50003pt}{(2s)!}\hskip 1.00006pt({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi)^{2s-\ell}(-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a)^{\ell}\right)\,,\\
&={\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}\left(e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006ptg^{+}_{\ell}\hskip-0.50003pt\hbar^{\ell}\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-0.50003pt\left(\frac{-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a}{\vphantom{\hbox{\raise 1.99997pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{${\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}$}}}}}}}{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi}\right)^{\kern-1.39304pt\ell}\left(\hskip-0.50003pt1-\frac{1}{(\ell-1)!}\hskip 1.00006pt\Gamma\big(\ell\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi+{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}a\big)\hskip-1.00006pt\kern-0.15pt\right)\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\right)\,.\end{split}

(196)

We have plugged in (
90
) to (
103a
)
and then placed
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathsf{3}}\cdot a}
in front of it by hand
while omitting
e_{h}\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x^{h}\hskip 1.00006pt
to avoid clutter.
In the large
{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt\vphantom{\psi}}\psi
limit, this agrees with the last line of (
192
)
so that (
193
)-(
194
) are reproduced.

It is not difficult to
understand
why this trick works
at the level of computation.
For a deeper reason,
it
might be plausible
to relate it to the fact that
the quantum amplitude of the minimally coupled particle, derived in (
204
),
is supported on
{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}={\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha}{}^{I}
.

Appendix C
Hamiltonian Perturbation Theory

Finally, we would like to comment that
it is also possible to develop the textbook perturbation theory
(which perturbs the Hamiltonian, not the symplectic structure)
for the massive twistor particle.
The purpose of this digression
is to complement our symplectic perturbation
approach
and point out possibilities of future applications,
e.g., applying the KMO’C formalism
[
13
]
.
We only
demonstrate
the simplest example:
scattering of a minimally coupled
fixed-mass
particle in the plus-helicity electromagnetic plane wave.

Canonical quantization

We first-quantize the massive twistor particle
in the reparameterization gauge (
23
)
where the Hamiltonian is
H={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}({\bar{\Delta}}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\Delta)+m_{0}

and treat it as a standard quantum-mechanical system
with “absolute time.”
In order to perform canonical quantization,
one should figure out what the canonical coordinates are.
A holomorphic electromagnetic field
A_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006ptdz^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}

translates to
-i\hskip 0.50003pt(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}A_{\smash{\alpha{\dot{{\gamma}}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt\rho^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}\hskip 0.50003ptdZ_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}

in the notation (
47
).
Taking
i\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{Z}}_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}dZ_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}
for the free-theory symplectic potential,
we find that the “kinetic” and “canonical” twistor variables are related as

\displaystyle(Z^{\text{kin}})_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt(Z^{\text{can}})_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I}\,,\quad({\bar{Z}}^{\text{kin}})_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt({\bar{Z}}^{\text{can}})_{I}{}^{\mathrm{A}}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006ptq(\lambda^{\text{kin}}{}^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}\hskip 1.00006ptA_{\smash{\alpha{\dot{{\gamma}}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z^{\text{kin}})\,(\rho^{\text{kin}})^{{\dot{{\gamma}}}{\mathrm{A}}}\,.

(197)

From now, we exclusively work with the canonical variables
(unlike in the main text)
and drop the “can” subscript to avoid clutter.
Then (
197
) boils down to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}&=(\lambda^{\text{kin}})_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,,\quad\mu^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}=(\mu^{\text{kin}})^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}\,,\\
{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}&=({\bar{\lambda}}^{\text{kin}})_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}-q(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\beta}A_{\smash{\beta{\dot{\alpha}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\,,\\
{\bar{\mu}}_{I}{}^{\alpha}&=({\bar{\mu}}^{\text{kin}})_{I}{}^{\alpha}-q(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\beta}A_{\smash{\beta{\dot{\alpha}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006ptz^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}\,.\end{split}

(198)

While
z^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}(\mu\lambda^{-1})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}(z^{\text{kin}})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
,

{\bar{z}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}{\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt}({\bar{\lambda}}^{-1}\hskip-0.24994pt{\bar{\mu}})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}
differs from
({\bar{z}}^{\text{kin}})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}

due to coupling to the holomorphic gauge field.

The Hamiltonian

H={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}({\rm det}({\bar{\lambda}}^{\text{kin}}){\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}{\rm det}(\lambda^{\text{kin}}))

gets perturbed in this canonical setting as

H=H^{\circ}+H^{\prime}
,
where
H^{\circ}={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}({\rm det}({\bar{\lambda}})+{\rm det}(\lambda))+m_{0}
and

H^{\prime}=H^{\prime}_{1}+H^{\prime}_{2}
:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{1}:=\frac{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{\Delta}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptqA_{\smash{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt(p{}^{-1})^{{\dot{\beta}}\beta}\,,\quad H^{\prime}_{2}:=-\frac{1}{4\Delta}\hskip 1.00006ptq^{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}A_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006ptA_{\smash{\beta{\dot{\beta}}}\vphantom{{\dot{\alpha}}}}\hskip-0.50003pt(z)\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\epsilon}}^{{\dot{\alpha}}{\dot{\beta}}}\,.

(199)

We restrict our attention to the leading order
and discard
H^{\prime}_{2}
.
For the self-dual plane wave (
112
), the
\mathcal{O}(q^{1})
interaction Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{1}=\frac{q}{\sqrt{2}\Delta}\hskip 1.00006pt\langle\iota|p|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\,{\mathrm{e}}^{-i\hskip 0.81949pt{\bar{o}}\mu\lambda^{-1}\hskip-0.40974pto}\,.

(200)

There are many kinds of eigenstates
in the free theory
that we can consider:
coherent states
{\big\langle{{\bar{Z}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}
and
{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{Z}\big\rangle}
,
frame eigenstates
{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}
,
and simultaneous eigenstates of
\hat{p}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}
and
\hat{\vec{W}}{}^{2}
,
which might be called “Wigner states.”
For our scattering problem, we identify
{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}
as the relevant in-state.
Using the convention

\{Z_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I},{\bar{Z}}_{J}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\}=(1/{i\hbar})\hskip 1.00006pt\delta_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\delta_{J}{}^{I}

\implies

\smash{[\hat{Z}_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{I},\hat{{\bar{Z}}}_{J}{}^{\mathrm{B}}]}=\delta_{\mathrm{A}}{}^{\mathrm{B}}\delta_{J}{}^{I}

(see footnote
10
),
the canonically quantized operators are realized as

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\lambda}_{\alpha}{}^{I}=\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}&\,,\quad{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{{\bar{\lambda}}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}={\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\,,\\
{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\mu}^{{\dot{\alpha}}I}=-i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}&\,,\quad{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{{\bar{\mu}}}_{I}{}^{\alpha}=-i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}{\big\langle{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\,.\end{split}

(201)

AHH amplitude from first quantization

From the interaction Hamiltonian (
200
),
the matrix element
is given by

\displaystyle{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hat{T}{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}

\displaystyle=-\hskip-1.00006pt\int d\tau\,\,{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{e}}^{-(\tau/{i\hbar})\hat{H}^{\circ}}{\hat{H}^{\prime}_{1}\,}{\mathrm{e}}^{(\tau/{i\hbar})\hat{H}^{\circ}}{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\,,

(202)

\displaystyle=\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(E^{\prime}{\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}E)\bigg[\hskip 0.24994pt\hskip 1.00006pt{-\frac{q}{\sqrt{2}\Delta}\hskip 1.00006pt\langle\iota|p|{\bar{o}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg({(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg)\hskip 0.50003pt{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg]+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\,,

where
E:={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}({\rm det}({\bar{\lambda}})+{\rm det}(\lambda))
,

E^{\prime}:={\textstyle\frac{1}{-2}}({\rm det}({\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime})+{\rm det}(\lambda^{\prime}))
,
and
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(\xi):=2\pi\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.50003pt\delta(\xi)
.
The exponential operator implements a translation of the right-handed spin frame:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt\bigg({(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}_{I{\dot{\alpha}}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}&\,=\,{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime},{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}{+}\lambda^{-1}{\mathsf{3}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}\,,\\
&\,=\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt(\lambda^{\prime}{\hskip 0.50003pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}\lambda)\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}+\lambda^{-1}{\mathsf{3}}-{\bar{\lambda}}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\,.\end{split}

(203)

When combined with
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(E^{\prime}{\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}E)
,
this product of delta functions
precisely describes the heavenly kinematics (
90
).
18
18

18

When dealing with delta functions of the complexified spinor-helicity variables and their determinants, we are implicitly working in the
(2,2)
signature.

Especially, one finds that
E^{\prime}{\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}E\hskip 0.50003pt=\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}p\hskip 1.00006pt/\Delta
.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\big\langle{\lambda^{\prime}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hat{T}{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda{\bar{\lambda}}}\big\rangle}=\sqrt{2}q\hskip 0.50003pt|\Delta|x\,\hskip 1.00006pt\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}p)\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt(\lambda{\hskip 0.50003pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}\lambda^{\prime})\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt\bigg({{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\lambda}}|{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\lambda}}|\frac{|{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|}{|\Delta|x}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\lambda}}{}^{\prime}|}\bigg)+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\,.

(204)

Observe how the first-quantized massive twistor operator algebra
in (
202
)-(
203
)
elegantly realizes Guevara, Ochirov, and Vines
[
8
]
’s exponential operator.
We can take (
204
) as a
straight-up derivation

of Arkani-Hamed, Huang, and Huang
[
3
]
’s
minimally coupled
on-shell amplitude
from the first-quantized theory.

To be precise, it is actually not yet on-shell,
albeit
the
x
-factor could be well-defined
in virtue of the delta function
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}p)=\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(E^{\prime}{\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}E)/\Delta
.
If we
finally
constrain the in- and out-states
to lie on
the mass shell,

\displaystyle(\hat{{\bar{\Delta}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m_{0}){\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{{\mathsf{1}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}}\big\rangle}=0\,,\quad(\hat{\Delta}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m_{0}){\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{{\mathsf{1}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}}\big\rangle}=0\,,

(205)

then
now
we can

identify

the matrix element with
[
3
]
’s amplitude:

\displaystyle{\big\langle{{\mathsf{2}}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt}\hat{T}{\hskip 0.50003pt\big|\hskip 0.50003pt{{\mathsf{1}}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}}\big\rangle}=\sqrt{2}q\hskip 0.50003ptm_{0}x\,\hskip 1.00006pt\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathsf{3}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\mathsf{1}})\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{|{\mathsf{1}}\rangle{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}|{\mathsf{2}}\rangle}\big)\,\delta^{(4)}\hskip-0.50003pt\bigg({{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}|{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{1}}}|\frac{|{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}{\kern-0.39993pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\hbox to0.0pt{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\rfloor$}}\hss}\rfloor}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.37003pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{3}}}|}{m_{0}x}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\kern-0.37003pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.99}[0.99]{{\hbox{{${\lfloor\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\lfloor$}}}}$}}}}}}}$}\kern-0.39993pt}{\bar{\mathsf{2}}}|}\bigg)+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\,.

(206)

We emphasize again that the amplitude is supported on the heavenly kinematics
so that it is indeed tempting to introduce the
on-shell
diagrammatic notations
(
90
)-(
90
).
Perhaps a certain realization of on-shell recursion in the massive twistor space
could take the heavenly kinematics as basic building blocks.

Going further, we can try to reproduce the impulses in section
3.3

by applying the KMO’C formalism
[
13
]

in this first-quantized setting.
Yet another direction
is non-minimal couplings.
Even though the relation between “kinematic” and “canonical” variables becomes unclear,
we could
work with higher-spin interaction Hamiltonians
constructed from real-spacetime fields.
We leave these topics for future research.
